   Lines are ordered among themselves the same way, by the collective's tips alone.

   **For the agreement in force at a record, only the records before it count** (B2). For an action, the records its history holds count, and those before them (F127, rule 58). A record it does not cite, a concurrent one included, decides nothing for it, save where it ends a power the action uses (the tie rule, point 7).

   *An act within its signer's powers counts as the signer knew the collective's head (F127).* *Read literally between two records drawn on two devices, each would count as after the other, each would judge the other's clone under the wrong parent, and A4's fork could never arise.*

2. **Others' acts are placed by the collective's.** An act of another identity done in the collective's name has no place of its own in the collective's sequence. A member's signature act is placed:

   - at the act of the collective it signs (C1);
   - at a record naming it among the signature acts that complete its clone (record field 2, A2);
   - at a rotation naming it among the signature acts that complete the clone it declares (rule 69, Flaw M);
   - at a recovery rotation naming it among the signature acts on a declaration that takes effect there (Flaw B18); and
   - at any act of the collective on its chain acknowledging it (Envelopes, `acks`) (C2).

   It counts as made before a line when one of those acts does, and as made after it otherwise.

   An Identity witness act of the collective (type 15), on neither chain, places nothing and counts for nothing in Agreements (F156).

   An act signed with a grant key (F128) is the collective's own, on the grantee's strand of its actions chain, citing its grant, as every action cites (F127, rule 58). It has no place by its key. It is before a line when the line's history holds it: that is, when an act of the collective before the line joined it, or a later act of the same strand that such an act joined. Where a decision ending the grant's powers does not hold it, the tie rule voids it (point 7), save where the collective itself adopts it (rule 76, A6).

3. **A departure takes effect at the collective's line** (A1, C3). A resignation, a stepping down, or a declaration removing a voice takes effect for the collective at the line registering it: a record naming it in field 3. A member's voice remains for an act of the collective made before that line, and through any signature of theirs placed before it. For everything after it, it is gone (rules 70, 71, 65).

   **Two lines neither of which is before the other are concurrent** (A4). Where a departure is registered on both, an act counts as made before it only when it is before both. Records on concurrent lines are a fork of the agreement chain (rule 72).

   A rotation declaring a clone that removes a party, or takes a holder off an area, is that change's own line (rule 69).

   Rarely, a declaration takes effect at a recovery rotation. A declaration takes effect at the collective's line, or, where the declared member is the one who would draw it (for instance the only holder of the collective's signing key), at the rotation made by the recovery path (rule 59), a line by its kept tips. That is the rotation that removes the declared member, declaring the clone that takes that party out of the parties, so that clone is counted without the declared party (B16). Where the declaration's authority is a threshold needing more than its own signer, that rotation names the others' signature acts and places them there (Flaw B18). The abandonment authority draws no line for the collective (C7).

   *A rotation names no declaration itself, only the signature acts completing one where its number needs them (B18); a recovery rotation declaring nothing new (Flaw B1) removes nobody, and leaves the declaration to the first record under the new key.*

4. **A member's own rotation is registered the same way** (C5). The collective registers a member's rotation (Identity) on its line, as it registers a departure (record field 3).

   A signature act of that member made with the key the rotation replaced, and placed before that line, stays valid for the collective, whatever the rotation kept or disowned, where it signs an act the collective's key holders also signed. That is an act of the collective, or a clone that a record or rotation of the collective puts in force, naming that signature among those that complete it (Q33, A2, Flaw M). Its status under Identity is unchanged, and every client shows that status.

   Such a signature placed after that line is judged by Identity alone (rule 6).

   *If the member's key was stolen, the thief's signatures placed before the line count too, but only where the collective's key holders also signed: a stolen member key can finish an act the collective signed, never start one; the collective then reorganizes around the theft.*

   *Stated cost (C5):* *within a collective, Identity's theft protection is softened to this extent.*

   *Stated cost (Q33):* *a signature on a clone that no act of the collective placed before the line registering its signer's rotation is judged by Identity alone, so one made from a device the rotation forgot no longer counts, as with C2; the collective's client avoids it by acknowledging members' signatures as they arrive.*

5. **The collective's keepers place its own acts a line left out** (A3, C4). An act of the collective that a line left out counts as made before that line when the keepers named by the collective's agreement in force for the line (Q34) recorded it before recording the line. This is as the keepers' own rule counts what is recorded: any one, a threshold, or all of them (rules 38, 41, 42; B9).

   Keepers place only the collective's own acts: never a member's signature, a grantee's act, or one line against another, which only the collective's tips order.

   *Stated cost:* *a keeper that records a line late opens a window, the trust in keepers rule 42 already states.*

6. **Nothing else places.** An acknowledgement by any other identity (Envelopes), a member's friend or a counterparty, carries no place in the collective's sequence: it keeps the act visible and changes nothing here (Flaw G). A member's personal sequences, and which device signed what, are never read for the collective.

7. **The tie rule** (F127). Where a decision ending powers and an action using them cite neither each other, **the ending wins: the action is void.** A decision ending powers is a fork, a closing, a departure, a grant's revocation, or a departure emptying an area (F128, reading 4 adjusted). So:

   - an act of the collective a fork's or closing's history does not hold counts for nothing (rule 91);
   - a member's voice a line removes is gone for every act the line's history does not hold, an act judged under a version earlier than the one the departure names included (rule 65);
   - an act signed with a grant key that a revocation of its grant, or the line emptying its grant's area, does not hold is void (rules 71, 76; G1, G2).

   *An action missing from the ending's history was made with powers that are ending; its counterparty deals again with a successor. Otherwise anyone holding a power could keep a fork or closing from settling by racing it. A counterparty protects itself by waiting until the actions chain visibly moves past its act before delivering.*

   **The tie rule voids only acts the collective never took on** (F131, IT2a). An act that an act of the collective's own key that counts cites is adopted (rule 76), and no ending racing that citation voids it. "Cites" is meant as a line cites: the act lies in the citing act's history, so citing a later act of the same strand cites it too (U3, F132).

   *A revocation from a device that had not heard of the citation cannot reach the act; it ends the grant for everything after it. A departure racing the citation takes no voice off the act: the act counts if it counts as judged, or once the departures racing its citation are set aside (F131, F132: whichever of the two judgments counts). Against a fork or closing nothing changes: an act citing it that the ending's history holds puts it in that history, and one the history does not hold is itself void (IT2b).*

   *Stated limit:* two acts signed at the same moment without seeing each other cannot be ordered by hashes alone; with two chains this remains only where a decision ending powers meets an action using them, and the tie rule settles it. Where the decision ends no power (a record of an operational or judicial clone), each act is judged under the decisions it cites (rule 58).

*Stated cost (F109):*

- *A departure takes effect for the collective's acts when the collective draws its line, not when the member signs it: until then, the departed member's signature still counts on the collective's acts. The line is drawn by whoever produces the collective's signature, who could already withhold it from any act.*
- *An act of the collective on a branch its own line left out counts as made after it, unless a keeper placed it: the cost falls on the collective, whose line it was, and on whoever relied on the act; it is the power its key holders already have at a rotation, which can leave out a branch (Identity rules 15 to 17).*
- *A member who left can still complete an act the collective signed before its line, and it counts: "the act was drafted with her in it" (C1). A pending act stays completable by whoever held the area when it was made.*
- *A signature the collective never acknowledged before its line, on a clone recorded only after it, no longer counts for its departed signer (C2); a collective's client SHOULD acknowledge members' signatures as they arrive, which keeps Q23's outcome.*

*Client conformance:* the collective's devices share their tips before a line is drawn, and a client warns before drawing one with a device not heard from, as before a rotation (Identity). **For a fork or a closing it refuses** (F131, IT2b): a member's client signs no fork or closing, its own or another member's, until it has pulled every device's latest acts and holds every act the ending's history should hold.

**In a deal, a declaration draws its own line** (Q28). A deal has no sequence of its own, and F109 does not reach it. An abandonment declaration with outcome 0 (rule 89) removes the party's voice in a deal from then on. Every signature act of that party on the deal counts as made after it, except one that the keepers the deal names recorded before recording the declaration, as their rule counts it (rules 38, 41, 42; B9). So a clone of the deal completed, and so recorded, before it is never undone. The other parties then change the deal without the declared party (rule 47).

*A deal's authority is one identity (Flaw B19): its signature alone makes the declaration, so the declaration is its own line, with nothing to complete after it.* *A signature on a deal's clone names the clone, an agreement, so the deal's keepers can place it; deals that need protection name a keeper.*

## Part four: act formats

All acts are Envelopes MIR acts, private by default, visible to the agreement's parties and keepers unless they choose otherwise.

Exact formats are set for: terms (type 0) and signatures (type 1), in the fields a collective needs and the tiers' fields, terms field 7 among them; grants, for the fields that tie them to an area (fields 5 and 6) and the grant key (field 9); revocations, resignations, records, abandonment declarations, negotiation messages, forks, closings, releases to the public domain and splits. The fields still marked open, and the other acts, are sketched, and their exact formats follow once the structure is settled. *A client that meets terms using a field whose format is still open refuses them rather than accepting them unchecked (Envelopes rule 11, fail closed).*

Every Agreements act type may carry acknowledgements (`acks`, Envelopes rules 4a and 7b, F110).

| Type | Name | Signed by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 0 | Terms | the proposer |
| 1 | Signature | each party (or grantee, or collective member) |
| 2 | Keeper record | a keeper's operator |
| 3 | Work claim | a sole creator; or one creator, who opens it, completed by the other creators' signature acts. Counts only public or addressed to every creator it names (step 12b) |
| 4 | Stake transfer | the seller, who also signs it by a chain signature (Identity type 16), which fixes its place on the seller's own line (F217); completed by the buyer's signature. Names the agreement as `[agreement, agreement]` |
| 5 | Public domain release | a direct owner of the work, completed by the signature acts of every other holder of the stakes it ends, unless the release rule says otherwise (field 24, F121). A collective owner signs by its own rules, across the lanes of Envelopes, Money and Agreements (F124 N7) |
| 6 | Standing offer | always public (F215). Under co-owners' agreement, the owners under its rule: in a deal every party, one signing it and the others by signature acts naming it (OF3 a); in a collective, an act of the collective. A lone seller's offer, by the seller, is itself the agreement with the buyer (F215) |
| 7 | *Retired* (step 12b, OF6 b): the payee's receipt naming the offer, and the claim where there is one, confirms delivery of a publication whose key is public; never reused | — |
| 8 | Split | the split service, with its own key; delivered to every holder it pays (F121, Q9) |
| 9 | Grant | the grantor. For a collective granting within an area, completed by its holders' signature acts; a reinstatement (field 7) likewise. A payee's grant in a deal: by the payee with its own key, carried by the deal that lists it (F129, H4). Accepted by the grantee's own signature act (F128) |
| 10 | Revocation | the grantor, with its own key, naming the grant whose key it removes (F128, C6). For a grant within an area, completed by that area's holders' signature acts (Q29) |
| 11 | *Retired* (step 12b, OF22 b): adoption is by acknowledging, citing or paying on (rule 76, H3, IT2a); never reused | — |
| 12 | Liveness | a party; counts only public or addressed to the agreement's other parties (OF23 a) |
| 13 | Abandonment declaration | the authority the clause names, always an identity (B12). For a threshold of the other parties (in a collective only, B19): one of them, completed by the others' signature acts (B15), which the recovery rotation names where the declaration takes effect there (B18) |
| 14 | Contest | anyone with standing (rule 54). Its format is written for the party a declaration of absence names, answering it (BQ4, F188), and for someone who signed onto a vow, contesting it (rule 13, F237, F240); the others are still open |
| 15 | *Retired* (F211): a Module states its fee in its own specification (Development field 7); never reused | — |
| 16 | Resignation | the member giving up their voice, or stepping down from one area, naming the drafts they leave behind (F207) |
| 17 | Record | the collective, with its signing key: its everyday line |
| 18 | Negotiation message | one side of a negotiation thread (rule 52, F118) |
| 19 | Fork | a member of the collective, with their own identity; completed by the chain signatures (Identity type 16) of every member it lists, meeting the constitutional change rule (F124 N1, F132), and by the signature acts of each successor a debt is assigned to (N13) (rule 91, F121, F140) |
| 20 | Closing | a member of the collective, with their own identity; completed by the chain signatures (Identity type 16) of members meeting the constitutional change rule (rule 91, F124 N9, F132, F140) |
| 21 | *Retired* (F126): the creditor's release is a Money act, Money type 4 (rule 92); never reused | — |
| 22 | Settlement request | a party who signed a deal's reference version, asking the judge of forks it names to settle the fork (rule 47; DQ8, F188) |
| 23 | Fork settlement | the judge of forks a deal's reference version names (field 27), or the link of its chain of judgment that took over (field 21), once a settlement request activated it (rule 47; DQ8, F188; QF2, F190; QG4) |
| 24 | Notice | a collective, with its own key, to a payer owed money back from a broken stretch who gave no address. Sealed to that payer, or public naming the payment where the payer committed only a one-time key (F208), with a deadline on a time reference (rule 93; F197) |
| 25 | Request to a judge | someone with standing (rule 54), naming a judge the terms name and the question. Counts, and the judge's period runs, only where public or sealed to that judge (rule 35; review 2.6) |

### Terms (type 0)

*In short: terms propose an agreement. The format below lists every field; the paragraphs after it are the checks that make terms invalid, and what each field means.*

```cddl
terms-payload = {
  0 => [+ hash],            ; parties: identity or collective hashes
  1 => tstr,                ; the terms' words, as canonical text; in a collective, the constitution's and the general
                            ;   words (each area's own words are in field 20)
  2 => { * uint => hash },  ; cMIPs, at most one per task (task number => cMIP hash); empty if it uses no task
  ? 15 => [+ hash],         ; extensions: cMIPs outside the listed tasks, which add rules and never relax the core's;
                            ;   in a collective, adopted in the Development lane (F106)
  ? 16 => [+ succession-plan] ; succession plans of parties, part of the signed terms
  ? 3 => keepers,           ; named keepers, and the rule for what counts as recorded
  4 => rule / mark,         ; founding terms: the signing rule, which signatures make the agreement exist: every party,
                            ;   in a deal (F107) and in a collective (Q11)
                            ; a clone: its mark, which change rules of the parent brought it into force, and by whom (F104)
  5 => rule,                ; clone rule: in a collective, for operational matters outside every area (the judicial tier
                            ;   needs every party whose voice remains, rule 67, F121); every party in a deal (F107)
  ? 18 => rule,             ; constitutional change rule; absent: every party (F103) (only with field 12)
  ? 19 => [+ area],         ; operational areas, powers of members (only with field 12)
  ? 20 => { + uint => tstr }, ; each area's own words, as canonical text, by area id (only with field 19)
  ? 6 => [ hash, any ],     ; time reference: the task cMIP and its parameters; a naming for task 10 (Q25), the same
                            ;   specification as field 2's task 10 where both are present (Q31)
  ? 7 => stakes,            ; stakes defined, in works, publications, or the collective itself (Q8), written null (S1)
  ? 8 => split-plan,        ; how incoming payments are divided ("The open formats", step 12b)
  ? 28 => fees,             ; fees, each entry borne by every stake, off the top: their own field (rule 28, F213);
                            ;   the form is under "Split plan and split" (text only: the field was missing here)
  ? 9 => abandonment,       ; the abandonment clause
  ; 10, the concurrency rule, is withdrawn (step 12b, OF20 c): rule 90's default stands; never reused
  ? 11 => hash,             ; parent: the agreement this one clones (with its latest act in objects)
  ? 12 => key-grammar,      ; present if this agreement founds a collective
  ? 13 => [+ hash],         ; arbitrators or verifiers named, who receive keys to judge content
  ? 14 => hash / [+ hash],  ; where incoming payments go to a split service: in a collective, its grant (one hash);
                            ;   in a deal, one grant per payee, each naming this agreement by null (F129, H4). Its one
                            ;   grantee is the agreement's designated pointer for payments, the same whoever pays
                            ;   (F248, rule 15): the first of the split cMIP's addresses, its backups following in
                            ;   field 29 (F250)
  ? 17 => refund-terms,     ; until when a refund owed on a payment under these terms is claimable: a point on the
                            ;   time reference (F219)
  ? 21 => [+ chain],        ; the chain of judgment: who takes over from a judge (rule 35, F121)
  ? 22 => [+ hash],         ; the departed members entry: who left the collective (rule 68, F121, F124 N5; only
                            ;   with field 12)
  ? 23 => hash,             ; forked from: in the founding terms of a fork's successor, the original collective, a
                            ;   back-link (rule 91, F124 N4; only with field 12)
  ? 24 => rule,             ; the release rule: which of a stake's holders sign its release (rule 55, F121); founding
                            ;   terms set it, and a clone every owner signs changes it (N8)
  ? 26 => [+ hash],         ; settles: in a deal's clone only, every tip of the deal's fork this version discards, that
                            ;   is, the latest version of each other branch, beside its one parent (field 11) on its own
                            ;   branch; ascending, none twice; each also cited in objects (rule 47; F186, F188,
                            ;   F189 5 and 6; F190 QF3)
  ? 27 => hash              ; the judge of forks: in a deal only, the identity among field 13 that settles the deal's
                            ;   forks on a party's request, following its chain of judgment (field 21) where it does
                            ;   not act; absent, none does (rule 47; DQ8, F188; QF2, F190; QG4)
  ? 29 => backups           ; the split service's backups, in order, and what counts as a failed attempt: fields of
                            ;   the split cMIP the agreement names (field 2's task 8, or the plan's key 1), signed with
                            ;   the terms (F249, F250, rule 17); only with field 14
}                           ; key 25 (the relays, F126) is withdrawn (F128) and never reused: terms carrying it are invalid

chain    = [ judge, next: [+ [ hash, period: uint ]] ]
                                      ; a judge the terms name, and in order those that take over from it, each with
                                      ;   the period, on the time reference, that the one before it has to act once
                                      ;   asked (Q7)
judge    = [ 0, task: uint ]          ; the specification named for judicial task 9, 10, 11 or 14 (field 2, field 6 for
                                      ;   10, or the abandonment clause's key 3 for 14; F183): next are specifications
         / [ 1, identity: hash ]      ; a keeper's operator, an arbitrator or verifier, or the abandonment authority the
                                      ;   terms name: next are identities
                                      ; [ 2 ], the split service (F121, F130 H6), is withdrawn (F249, F250) and never
                                      ;   reused: terms carrying it are invalid; its backups are field 29

backups  = {                          ; terms field 29 (F249, F250)
  0 => [+ hash / [+ hash]],           ; each backup split service after field 14's, in order: in a collective, its
                                      ;   grant; in a deal, its grants, one per payee, each naming this agreement by
                                      ;   null, as many as field 14 lists (H6's grants per payee)
  ? 1 => any                          ; what counts as a failed attempt: the split cMIP's parameters for it
}

key-grammar = {
  0 => holding,             ; how the signing key is held
  1 => holding,             ; how the chain key is held, usually stricter
  ? 3 => recovery           ; a way to rotate that needs less than every member; required when holding 1 names
                            ;   every member
}                           ; key 2 (once: listed act types) is retired and never reused: areas reach acts (field 19)
holding  = [ 0, holder: hash ]                        ; one holder
         / [ 1, threshold: uint, members: [+ hash] ]  ; any k of these members: jointly for the signing key; by
                                                      ;   shares for the chain key
         / [ 2, custodian: hash, grant: hash ]        ; a custodian under the collective's grant
recovery = [ 0, custodian: hash, grant: hash ]        ; a custodian holds a share under grant
         / [ 1, authority: hash ]                     ; an escrowed share released by the abandonment authority

succession-plan = {
  0 => hash,                               ; the party whose succession this is
  ? 1 => [+ [ successor: hash, share: uint ]],   ; stake successors, shares in millionths of the party's stakes
  ? 2 => [+ [ successor: hash, weight: uint ]],  ; seat successors, with their voting weight
  ? 3 => uint                              ; seat entry: 0 automatic where every voice signed this version of the
                                           ;   plan, otherwise treated as 1 (Q14); 1 a nomination, approved under the
                                           ;   power a change of parties needs (rule 64)
}

abandonment = {
  0 => [ 0, hash ]          ; the authority: a named identity (a keeper's operator, a party, a collective, or a third party)
     / [ 1, uint ],         ;   or a threshold of the other parties: in a collective only (B19)
  1 => [+ uint],            ; outcomes allowed (rule 89), ascending: 0 voice removed (F105), 1 stake redistributed,
                            ;   2 stake transferred to parties named or defined by role, 3 obligations redirected or
                            ;   held, 4 agreement closed
                            ; key 2 (the period of absence, F140) is retired (F172) and never reused: terms carrying
                            ;   it are invalid
  ? 3 => [ hash, any ]     ; an absence-proof cMIP and its parameters (a period, a time reference), task "Absence proof" (F172)
}

keepers   = [ [+ hash], rule ]                      ; keeper operators, and any one / threshold / all
rule      = [ 0 ] / [ 1, uint ] / [ 2, [+ hash] ]   ; all parties / threshold / named parties
stakes    = [+ [ object: who, [+ [ holder: who, share: uint ]] ] ]
                                                    ; shares in millionths, summing to 1,000,000; leftovers by
                                                    ;   largest remainder, the order of holders deciding nothing
                                                    ;   (F150); each stake is identified by its index here;
                                                    ;   an object once; holders distinct, each share above zero;
                                                    ;   the object the collective itself: a share of all its
                                                    ;   income (Q8); the object a vow's name: held by the
                                                    ;   vow's signer (null for the collective's own vow) in
                                                    ;   the version first writing it (new in draft 13, F284;
                                                    ;   rule 14)
who       = hash / null                             ; an identity, or null: this collective, only in a collective's terms (S1)

area = {
  0 => tstr,                ; its name, as canonical text, shown before signing
  1 => [* hash],            ; holders: parties; empty only in a clone, the area then standing frozen (Q21)
  2 => uint,                ; how many of the holders decide together: at least 1, and at most the number of holders,
                            ;   except in a clone that keeps the number the parent's entry for that area has, which
                            ;   may then exceed the holders, counted as rule 65 says (Q26)
  ? 3 => [+ kind],          ; the acts of the collective it reaches
  ? 4 => [+ field-ref],     ; the operational fields of the terms it reaches
  5 => uint                 ; its id: permanent, unique among the agreement's areas, never reused (Q32)
}                           ; an area reaches at least one act kind or one field; it is identified by its id, never
                            ;   by its place in field 19
kind = [ 0, layer: uint ]                 ; every act of this layer ("Layer", F106): 0 Identity, 1 Envelopes and Text,
                                          ;   2 Money, 3 Agreements, 4 Development
     / [ 1, spec: hash, type: uint ]      ; every act of this type in this specification
field-ref = [ 0, field: uint ]            ; a whole operational field: 7 stakes, 8 split plan, 17 refund terms
          / [ 1, task: uint ]             ; one entry of field 2: the cMIP for this task, where the task is operational

mark  = [+ [ power, signers: [+ hash] ] ] ; each change rule claimed, and the parties whose signatures meet it (Q7);
                                          ;   ascending by the power's deterministic encoding, no power twice;
                                          ;   each power's signers ascending by hash, none twice (B8)
power = [ 0 ]                             ; the parent's constitutional change rule (its field 18, else every party)
      / [ 1 ]                             ; the parent's clone rule (its field 5)
      / [ 2, area: uint ]                 ; the power over the parent's area of this id
      / [ 3, party: hash ]                ; the succession plan of this party, signed in advance by every voice
                                          ;   (rule 83, Q14); its signers are the seat successors entering
      / [ 4 ]                             ; the judicial tier's rule: every party of the parent whose voice remains
                                          ;   (rule 67, F121)
```

*A rule's threshold is between 1 and the number of parties; named parties are parties. In a key grammar, members of a holding are parties.*

*An area's holders are distinct parties. A clone that takes a party out of field 0 takes it out of every area's holders too, and founding terms never list an area without holders (Q21). A clone that takes a holder off an area need not lower its number: a number left above the holders that remain is met by all of them (rule 65, Q26), and the refit sets it again. A clone that changes an area's number sets it within its holders.*

*The signers of each power in a mark are distinct, listed ascending by hash, so that one meaning has one encoding (B8); a mark listing them otherwise is invalid. A threshold is written as the founders chose it; how it counts once voices are removed is rule 65.*

**A clone** is terms with a parent (field 11).

- Its inside names, in `objects`, the parent's chain and the act of that chain it follows: `[[parent, act]]`.
- **A settling version** (field 26) **also names the version it settles, as chain and predecessor**: `[[parent, act], [settled, settled]]`, so that a verifier fetching a deal's versions by their citations finds it (F189, 6). Terms carrying field 26 that do not are invalid.
- Founding terms name no chain.
- A clone's field 4 is a mark; founding terms' field 4 is a rule. Terms whose field 4 has the other form are invalid.

**Founding terms exist only when every party has signed them,** a deal's and a collective's alike (F107, Q11). Founding terms whose field 4 is not `[0]` are invalid. *A person is never added to a collective without their approval: a founder who has not signed leaves the collective unfounded, and a member added later joins only by signing the clone that adds them (rule 45).*

**A deal** (terms without field 12) has every party as its clone rule too. Any deal whose field 5 is not `[0]` is invalid, and so are terms of a deal carrying field 18, 19 or 20 (F107). *The fields are kept rather than dropped so that every agreement has one format, and a deal says on its face what it needs.*

**In a deal, the absence authority is one identity** (Flaw B19): a party, a keeper's operator or a collective, named by the clause's first form, `[0, hash]`. Terms of a deal whose abandonment clause names a threshold of the other parties, `[1, uint]`, are invalid, whatever its number. *A deal has no sequence of its own in which several signatures could be placed against its line. Parties who need to decide absence together form a collective, which orders its own acts on its own sequence (F109), and name it as the authority: "a collective made of collectives".*

**A judge never handles what it judges** (Q20). This holds in every agreement, a deal's as a collective's (Q24).

A judge here is a specification that is either:

- named in field 2 for a judicial task (9 condition evaluation, 10 time reference, 11 anchoring); or
- named in field 6 as the time reference, which is a naming for task 10 (Q25).

A judge is named for no other task, judicial or not, and is not named as an extension (field 15). Terms that do either are invalid, founding terms and clones alike, whatever a clone's mark says.

*"It removes a slight conflict of interest." An extension fills no task, but it adds rules to acts; one that is also a judge would handle what it judges, so every way a specification can be named is covered (Q25). In a deal every party signs every cMIP, so no lane can slip a judge in; the reason is the judge's, not the lanes' (Q24). It also keeps R4 away from the judges: no specification serves a judicial task and a lane's task at once, so no lane's holders ever have a say over a judge, and no change to a judge reaches a lane's acts.*

**The chain of judgment** (field 21, F121) says who takes over from a judge the terms name. The judges it may follow are:

- a judicial task the terms name a specification for;
- an identity the terms name as a keeper's operator, an arbitrator or verifier, or the abandonment authority (its first form).

The checks:

- Each judge has one link at most, the links ascending by the judge's deterministic encoding.
- Those that take over from a judge are distinct, and none is the judge itself.
- **A specification that takes over from a judicial task is a judge too, so it is named nowhere else in the terms**: for no task, as no extension, and in no other link (Q20, Q25).
- Each link names a period above zero (Q7): the time the one before it has, once asked, to act, measured on the agreement's time reference. After it, the next may act.
- Terms whose chain follows a judge that can stay silent (an identity) name a time reference (field 6, or field 2's task 10). That time reference is itself a judge, which may have a chain of its own.

Terms breaking any of these are invalid. How a chain passes is rule 35.

*A period is compulsory for every link, a specification's included, though a specification always answers. "With compulsory time reference" (Nobody, allegedly, Q7).*

*The chain is signed with the terms, by everyone: in a deal by every party, in a collective as part of the judicial tier (rule 67). So nobody signs again when a judge fails.*

*The split service is not in the chain (F249, F250): its backups are the split cMIP's, in field 29, below. "A judge chain has stages. A back up chain has attempts if previous one fails. Everything reverts back if attempts 1 succeeds" (Nobody, allegedly).*

**The split service's backups** (field 29, F249, F250; rule 17).

*How it runs most of the time:* an agreement names one split service (field 14) and no backups; every payment goes to it.

Where the owners choose backups, **field 29 lists them, in order, after field 14's service, with what counts as a failed attempt.** These are fields of the split cMIP the agreement names (field 2's task 8, or the split plan's key 1; where both name one, the same), signed with the terms by everyone who signs them. The checks:

- Field 29 appears only with field 14, and only where the terms name a split cMIP.
- In a collective, each backup is one grant of the collective.
- In a deal, each backup is a group of grants, one per payee, as many as field 14 lists, each the payee's own grant naming "this agreement" by null like field 14's (H6's grants per payee, F130).
- Every grant that field 14 and field 29 list is listed once.

Terms breaking any of these are invalid. The core never reads key 1: what counts as a failed attempt is the split cMIP's. *Attempts, not stages:* no period and no time reference are needed (F249: F246's stages and deadlines do not apply to backups).

**The departed members entry** (field 22, F121, F124 N5) appears only in terms carrying a key grammar. It lists identities:

- distinct;
- none of them a party;
- each a holder of a share of the stake in the collective itself (field 7).

Terms breaking any of these are invalid. The entry records only that each is departed, without voice (rule 68); the stakes decide what every holder is paid. *"They are stakeholders. So the stakes still show them, but the organizational part files the ID as departed" (Nobody, allegedly).*

**Stakes** (field 7, Q8). Each stake:

- names its object once;
- has distinct holders, each share above zero;
- has shares summing to 1,000,000 (rule 9).

Terms breaking any of these are invalid.

**Null names this collective** (F124, S1). A stake whose object is null is a share of all the collective's income. A holder written null is the collective itself, holding that stake. Only terms carrying a key grammar write null. A stake in the collective held by the collective itself is invalid. Founding terms so carry the members' stakes in the collective, and the stakes of the works it owns, from its birth.

*"Let's reuse the mechanic" (Nobody, allegedly): the collective's identity is its genesis act, which declares the founding agreement, so the founding terms could not name it by its hash. In its own terms, founding or clone, the collective is written null, never by its hash, so that one meaning has one encoding (as B8); a clone writing the collective's own hash is invalid where a verifier holds its genesis (F125).*

**Forked from** (field 23, F121, F124 N4) appears only in the founding terms of a collective (with field 12, without field 11). It holds the original collective's identity. It is a back-link for readers and decides nothing: the fork act names its successors (rule 91). It is no parent: the founding terms are signed by every founder, as any founding terms are (Q11). Terms breaking this are invalid. *A successor is founded before the fork act exists, so it cannot name the act; it names the collective it leaves.* *Reading, to confirm:* *the collective, rather than its agreement, which may still change before the fork.*

**The release rule** (field 24, F121, F124 N8) says which of a stake's holders must sign its release to the public domain, a rule counted among them (rule 55). It is set in an agreement's founding terms; absent, every holder.

**A clone every owner signs may change it.** A clone whose field 24 differs from its parent's is a draft until every holder of every stake the parent defines has signed it (rule 45). This is besides the power its mark names: **the ordinary clone rule**, field 24 lying in no area (rule 64; F125, D4).

*"Freedom of making mistakes and fixing them" (Nobody, allegedly).* *Reading, to confirm:* *the owners are the holders the parent's field 7 names, a holder written null being the collective, whose consent the mark already carries.*

**Field 25 is withdrawn** (F128). Terms carrying field 25 are invalid, and the number is never reused. **Relays are transport**: where a collective's clients publish and look first is client conformance, or a cMIP's (the relay transport cMIP), never the core (rule 57).

*Field 25 named a collective's relays, and made an act done only once it was on one of them. That made an act's validity depend on infrastructure: a vanished or hostile operator could freeze a collective, even its decision to change relays; it gave leverage over the collective; it allowed omission to readers checking one relay; and it failed offline verifiers. That crosses MOR's line that an act is valid by its signatures and content wherever held. The relays were a witness of time; the two chains and the tie rule (F127) answer "before" without them.*

**An agreement has one time reference** (Q31). Where terms carry both field 6 and a cMIP for task 10 in field 2, the two name the same specification, field 6 adding its parameters. Terms where they differ are invalid, founding terms and clones alike. *One check, no change of format: terms that use either place alone stay valid.*

**Areas** (field 19) appear only in terms that carry a key grammar (field 12) (F103, scope). Area words (field 20) appear only for an area that exists.

**No two areas reach the same act or the same field** (Q5). Two kinds overlap when some act could belong to both, as computed from the six MIR hashes and the terms' own fields 2 and 15 ("Layer"). So:

- `[0, layer]` overlaps `[1, spec, type]` when `spec` is a MIR of that layer, a cMIP the terms name for a task of that layer, or, for Development, an extension they name;
- a lane overlaps a field reference to a task of its own layer, since the lane already decides that task's cMIP (rule 63).

The one exception is a specification the terms name for tasks of two layers or more, never a judicial task (Q20). Its acts belong to each of those layers, and are reached by the lane of each, which then decide them together (rule 60, R4).

A field reference names an operational field or task ("Tiers").

Genesis and rotations are never in an area's reach: the key grammar governs them. Nor are records (type 17), judged by the clone they name. An area whose kind names a genesis or a rotation (Identity's chain acts) or a record is invalid, never read as reaching nothing (B7).

Terms breaking any of these are invalid.

**An area is followed by its id** (Q32). The ids of an agreement's areas (area key 5) are distinct. An area of a clone is the parent's area with the same id, wherever it sits in field 19. Grants (field 5), area words (field 20), steppings down (resignation field 1), marks (`[2, area]`) and a refit (rule 71) name an area by its id. A clone may drop an area, and adds one only under an id that no earlier version of the agreement, back to its founding terms, gave to an area. Terms breaking either are invalid. *An area keeps its id through any redraw, so removing or reordering areas never moves a grant, words or a refit onto another area.*

*Which layer an act belongs to is computed from its own `spec` field, the six MIR hashes and the collective's own terms, never from a label the cMIP's author chose (F106). A collective that gives Money to some members gives them the Money MIR's own acts, its receipts and claims on every rail among them, and the acts of every Money cMIP it adopted, by its own list. A new rail Module needs no clone at all: the collective's payee pointer, an act of its Money lane, or its vault names it, and a receipt counts only on a rail Module so named (Money rule 12a, F115).*

*An act of a specification the terms name nowhere falls in no lane. In a collective with areas it counts for nothing, unless a `[1, spec, type]` kind of some area names it (Q16), since otherwise an unlisted specification would be a way around every lane.*

*A specification named for tasks of two layers belongs to both. Where the two lanes are held by different areas, each lane's holders must sign its acts, as for a clone across two areas (Q7), and naming it for the second layer needs both lanes' power (rule 63), so neither lane's holders can bind the other's acts alone (R4).*

*Stated cost:* *a collective cannot give its releases to one member and its other publications to another by lane alone, since both are Envelopes publications; a `[1, spec, type]` kind still names a single type (F106).*

### Signature (type 1)

*In short: a signature act is one identity's consent to an act that needs it.*

```cddl
signature-payload = {
  0 => hash                 ; the act signed: terms, a clone, an act of a collective an area reaches, a grant within an area,
                            ;   or an abandonment declaration whose authority is a threshold of the other parties (B15)
}
```

The inside names, in `objects`, the act signed as both chain and predecessor: `[[signed, signed]]`. *A signature follows the act it signs.*

Signatures naming one act are parallel consents to it, never a fork among themselves: rule 6's forks are between acts that change an agreement.

A signature counts only while it is valid under Identity's rules. The exception is a signature a collective placed before its line registering its signer's rotation (rule 6, C5).

### Standing offer (type 6)

*In short: a standing offer sells something at one price to whoever pays, until it is withdrawn or its time runs out. A lone seller makes it alone; co-owners make it under their agreement.*

*In the open formats of step 12b (standing offer, refund terms, work claim, stake transfer, request to a judge, liveness), a choice marked "(mechanic, the build's)" was taken under the delegation of mechanics, not made by a decision (F207 to F219). Numbers of new types and fields there are technical choices.*

```cddl
offer-payload = {
  ? 0 => hash,          ; under: the co-owners' agreement (a version) it is made under; present only where co-owners stand
                        ;   behind it (F215); absent: a lone seller's offer, itself the agreement with the buyer
  1 => [* sold],        ; what it sells, ascending by deterministic encoding, none twice; empty only in a later version,
                        ;   which withdraws the offer (OF4 a; mechanic, the build's)
  2 => amount,          ; the price, one amount (OF5 a)
  ? 3 => hash / 0,      ; who is paid: a payee, or 0, the stakes, each holder's own pointer (rule 16; review 2.1); required
                        ;   with field 0 (mechanic, the build's); absent with no field 0: the signer
  ? 4 => tstr,          ; the offer's words, canonical text; required where it names a vow (F237)
  ? 5 => any,           ; until: a point on the time reference after which it takes no payment
  ? 6 => [hash, any],   ; a lone seller's own time reference (F215, F219; mechanic, the build's)
  ? 7 => refund-terms   ; a lone seller's own refund terms (F215; mechanic, the build's)
}
sold = [ 0, publication: hash ]       ; a publication (Envelopes type 0), by its act id
     / [ 2, [ cmip: hash, any ] ]     ; a proven offer: a delivery whose proof any verifier checks from data alone, under a
                                      ;   cMIP and its parameters (widened from access to bytes, F261; a stream's key alone, F264)
     / [ 3, vow: hash ]               ; a vow (Envelopes type 5), by its name, its genesis id (F237)
```

`[ 1, work ]` (any publication carrying a work) is not written (question QJ3, with F216).

Its `objects`: a later version names `[first, previous]` first (OF4 a). Where field 0 is present, they name the agreement as `[agreement, agreement]`; a collective's offer then cites its chain (F127).

**It counts** where:

- it is valid and public (F215);
- under a deal, it is signed by every party (OF3 a);
- its field 3 is a payee the agreement's field 14 lists (in a collective, the collective), or, where the agreement names no split service, the stakes (review 2.1 to 2.3);
- a lone seller's payee is the signer, or one who accepted it by a signature act (Money rule 14);
- field 5 needs a time reference.

**A purchase following it** names its agreement (a version of its lineage), or it is no purchase. A payment following a lone seller's offer names no claim and is accepted under the offer's terms (F215); one the time reference places past field 5 is owed back.

A split service's grant key may receipt under an offer that pays its grantor under the grantor's own claim (H5's reading 4, lifted).

**A relay's delivery record counts on a purchase naming an offer** where its object is the media of a publication the offer sells (Fable's 4b; mechanic, the build's: any of them).

**An offer naming a vow** (`[ 3, vow ]`, F237) sells it with its words, under whatever agreement claims it, or as a lone seller's offer. It names the vow by its genesis id, never a later version. An act this verifier holds that is not a vow, or is a later version, keeps the offer from counting. A vow made void by its signer's rotation does not: the name others wrote down stands (F237). *What the vow promises, how it is delivered, quantities and seats, and what it became are the cMIPs' (F237); the form number is the build's mechanic.*

**A service sold under a cMIP's terms** (F225; written in at step 14a's second pass, `docs/anchoring-second-pass.md`, for Nobody, allegedly, to approve again). *"Paying for anchoring is an agreement" (Nobody, allegedly).* A lone seller's offer selling access under a cMIP (`[ 2, [ cmip, terms ] ]`) carries that cMIP's terms as its own. **New in draft 11 (F261):** the access form is the form of every proven offer, any delivery whose proof a verifier checks from data alone (rule 11); the verifier checks the proof, never the cMIP's say-so. Their deadlines are points on the offer's own time reference (field 6). What a default owes (a refund of the price, to the payer the payment committed to) is judged under them, by a client that holds that cMIP. Its refund terms (field 7) end a refund unpaid past their point (F219).

The pooled anchoring service sells so, a proven offer, unchanged (anchoring cMIP draft 3: its tiers, prices or price scheme, and deadlines; tickets and batch publications its acts under the offer). Money carries the refund's payment and shows it repaid (Money rules 10a and 14). *Money says how money moves and that it has moved; Agreements say what must happen, in return or in consequence (F225).*

**Refund terms (terms field 17, an offer's field 7).** `refund-terms = { 0 => [ 0, point: any ] }`: the refund is claimable until this point on the time reference (F219, OF7 a). Terms carrying it name a time reference (rule 33). Past the point, the refund is ended (rule 11).

### Work claim (type 3)

*In short: a work claim ties a work, by its hash, to its creators.*

```cddl
work-claim-payload = {
  0 => hash,            ; the work hash
  1 => [+ hash],        ; the creators, ascending, none twice, the signer (who opens it) among them
  ? 2 => [hash, any]    ; a work-claim cMIP and what it produces (a pre-publication commitment)
}
```

It is bound when every other creator listed has a signature act naming it. It counts only public or addressed to every creator (OF16 a). It carries no roles (OF17 a). Where no agreement writes shares, the default holder is the creator who opened it (F218).

### Stake transfer (type 4)

*In short: a holder hands all or part of a stake to a new holder.*

```cddl
transfer-payload = {
  0 => hash,            ; the version of the agreement defining the stake
  1 => uint,            ; the stake, by its index there
  2 => hash,            ; to: the new holder
  3 => uint,            ; how much, in millionths of the stake: above zero, at most what the seller still holds at its place on its own line (F224)
  ? 4 => [+ hash]       ; what the seller took for it: a payment made before it; read only to find what an over-sale owes back (F224)
}
```

Its `objects` name the agreement as `[agreement, agreement]`, never a fork.

It takes effect once the seller's chain signature names it and the buyer's signature act completes it (rule 10, F217), where it fits what the seller still held at that place (F224). An over-sale confers nothing.

Across versions, a stake is followed by its object (FR7). A payment for it fulfils it (Money field 5) or is named in its field 4 (mechanic, the build's).

### Split plan and split (type 8)

*In short: the split plan (terms field 8) says how money reaching the owners is divided; a split (type 8) is the split service's act dividing one payment, payout by payout, which anyone can check against the plan. Fees are their own terms field, 28.*

```cddl
split-plan = {                ; terms field 8
  0 => [+ share-rule],      ; stakes, then roles, then named receivers, then metric shares; every share but a
                            ;   stake's is named in key 4, off the top (new in draft 12, F280)
  1 => hash,                ; split cMIP, which declares the remainder rule and computes the amounts (OF8 b)
  ; 2 is not used: fees are terms field 28, their own field (F213)
  ? 3 => unfilled,          ; what happens to a role share nobody fills; absent: [ 0 ], to the stakes (F218)
  ? 4 => [+ uint],          ; indexes of shares taken off the top, before the rest is divided; the rest goes to
                            ;   the stakes alone, by their parts (new in draft 12, F280)
  ? 5 => uint,              ; who bears payout rail fees: 0 each receiver (default), 1 the split service
  ? 6 => amount / uint,     ; maximum fee per payout the service may deduct: absolute, or in millionths of the payout;
                            ;   absent: no cap, flagged before signing (rule 25, F271)
  ? 7 => any,               ; the split cMIP's parameters (a waterfall's thresholds)
  ? 8 => [+ metric]         ; the metrics the plan divides by (rule 29)
}
share-rule = [ 0, stake: uint, part: uint ]         ; a stake, by its index in field 7; paid to its current holders
           / [ 1, role: tstr, part: uint ]          ; a role, by its name, filled at payment time (review 7.2)
           / [ 2, receiver: hash, part: uint ]      ; a named receiver that holds no stake (a service, a position)
           / [ 3, metric: uint, part: uint ]        ; a metric share, by its index in field 8
metric     = [ module: hash, measurer: hash, ? params: any ]   ; the measurer named in advance (rule 29)
unfilled   = [ 0 ] / [ 1 ] / [ 2, party: hash ]     ; to the stakes pro rata / held open / to a named party
fees       = [+ fee]                                ; terms field 28 (F213)
fee        = [ module: hash, part: uint, ? scope ]  ; borne by every stake, off the top (rule 28)
scope      = [ 0, publication: hash ]               ; payments for this publication; absent: every payment (F214)
           / [ 1, rail: hash ]                      ; payments by this rail Module

split-payload = {
  0 => hash,                ; the receipt or payer's claim that brought the money to the owners' payee pointer
  1 => [+ payout],          ; each payout, summing exactly to the amount received
  2 => hash,                ; the split cMIP used
  3 => hash,                ; the owners' agreement whose stakes it pays (F121)
  ? 4 => [+ [ stake: uint, [+ [ holder: hash, count: uint ]] ]],  ; the running count of leftover units (rule 9, F165)
  ? 5 => uint,              ; its number: one numbering across every split the service makes under a deal, any branch, from 1 (DQ6)
  ? 6 => [+ hash],          ; the Modules and cMIPs it ran under (rule 28): the service's statement, never what decides (F214)
  ? 7 => [+ hash],          ; the metric records it divides by (rule 29)
  ? 8 => [+ [ stake: uint, transfer: hash ]]   ; per stake, the latest transfer it follows: the service's statement (OF19 a, F217)
}
payout = {
  0 => hash,                ; the receiver
  1 => uint,                ; the amount, in the smallest part of the receipt's unit
  ? 2 => uint,              ; the stake it pays, by its index in the agreement's field 7
  ? 3 => tstr,              ; the role it fills
  ? 4 => hash,              ; the evidence for that role: an act signed by someone other than the split service and the payee,
                            ;   or, for a rail Module's role share, the receipt or claim naming that rail Module, where the
                            ;   payee's own pointer or vault names it (rule 22, F119); only with key 3
  ? 5 => hash,              ; a fee: the module whose fee it pays, its receiver being key 0 (Q9); never with key 2 or 3
  ? 6 => uint,              ; the rail fee deducted, within the plan's maximum
  ? 7 => uint               ; the metric share it pays, by its index in plan field 0 (rule 29); never with keys 2, 3 or 5
}
```

**Fees (terms field 28).** Fees are their own terms field, not a key of the plan (F213); their form is `fees` above. An area may reach field 28 apart from field 8 (F213, `field-ref` `[0, 28]`).

**Every payout matches its stake exactly** (F124, N10, rule 27). For each stake a split pays, each holder receives its share of what the split pays that stake, exactly as rule 9 sends each leftover unit under the default rule (F175, F178), and nobody else receives on it.

A holder that is another agreement receives at the one pointer that agreement names for money arriving from above (rule 15, F244). The plan never divides that holder's share among the next agreement's owners.

Every fee is taken before the stakes divide, so it falls alike on every stake. Any deviation, either way, breaks the plan, and is shown so. *No holder is paid a whole smallest unit or more below its exact share, and none above it by as many units as the stake has holders or more, which leaves room for the leftovers of rounding going to one holder (rule 21; F125 reading 7).*

Each payout is then paid as a simple Money payment, with its own receipt naming the split. A fee taken off the top, and an app share for the client that brought the sale (a role share), are both expressed in the plan.

**What a verifier checks against the plan:**

- a role payout fills a role the plan names; several fillers of one role are paid equally, within one unit (OF11 a);
- a named receiver's payout is one the plan names;
- a fee payout is a fee entry's, and every fee an entry makes owed on the payment is paid (F214);
- a metric payout names a metric share; its receiver is one the plan already pays, never the metric's measurer (review 2.7); the measurer is not the split service; and the split names a record of the metric's Module signed by the measurer (OF13 d: the record's format is the Module's);
- a payout on a stake whose holder is another agreement names, as its receiver, the one pointer that agreement designates for payments (rule 15, F235, F244, F248): field 14's one grantee, or a lone owner; or, for that payment, a backup of its field 29 once every attempt before it failed, as its split cMIP defines failure (rule 17, F249, F250).

A holder designating none cannot be paid, and any payout on its holding to anyone else is a mismatch: **never an address the owners did not sign**.

How much each share gets is the split cMIP's (OF8 b): a verifier without it shows the amounts as unknown, never as wrong.

**A split is delivered to every holder it pays** (Q9): each receiver of a payout naming a stake is among its recipients (Envelopes, `to`), unless the split is public (*Reading, to confirm: a public split is delivered to everyone*). Every fee it takes is a payout naming its module and its receiver. *The core cannot know who owns a service, so it never hides a fee: every holder sees what was taken before their share, and by whom: "legible and poetically public" (Nobody, allegedly).*

### Grant (type 9) and revocation (type 10)

*In short: a grant lets a grantee act for the grantor, within limits, with a key of the grantee's own that is scoped to the grant. A revocation removes that key.*

```cddl
grant-payload = {
  0 => hash,                ; grantee
  1 => uint,                ; scope: 0 sign new deals, 1 manage named existing ones, 2 act for an identity (post, publish, spend)
  ? 2 => [+ hash] / null,   ; the agreements concerned, for scope 1; null: this agreement, the deal whose terms list the grant
                            ;   in field 14 (F129, H4); null only with scope 1, never with field 5, 7 or 8
  ? 3 => any,               ; limits: per-deal cap, total, spending, as the grant's cMIP defines
  ? 4 => hash,              ; the cMIP defining the limits
  ? 5 => uint,              ; for a collective: the area whose power issues it, by its id in the agreement in force at the grant's binding (Q32)
  ? 6 => [+ kind],          ; the acts it reaches; required with field 5, and within that area's reach
  ? 7 => hash,              ; a reinstatement: the ended grant it reinstates, fields 0 to 6 repeating that grant's (rule 71, Flaw N); only with field 5
  ? 8 => null,              ; the grantor is this collective, the one whose founding terms name the grant (S1); never with field 5 or 7
  9 => signing-key          ; the grant key (F128), in Identity's form [ scheme, key ]: made and held by the grantee, scoped to this grant
}

revocation-payload = {
  0 => hash                 ; the grant whose key it removes (F128)
}
```

**The grant key** (field 9, F128). The grant names the public part of a signing key the grantee made and keeps (reading: the grantee makes it, so nobody else holds a key that acts as the grantee's within the collective). **The grant counts only once its grantee signed a signature act naming it** (type 1), accepting it, a grant by founding terms included (reading, F128).

An act signed with the grant key names the grant as its `binding` (Identity: a scoped key, which this MIR judges), and its signer is the collective. It is the collective's own act, on the grantee's strand of the collective's actions chain, which cites the grant in `objects` as the decision it acts under (rule 58). It is judged at the latest link of the collective's identity chain among its grant and the decisions it cites (reading).

A publication a grantee makes with its grant key is the grantor's own, signed by it, so payment reaches the grantor whatever client pays (F128); it needs no `for` field.

An act of the grant key is backed (rule 80) only within the grant's reach (field 6, or, for a grant naming no area, acts no area reaches), and only while the grant has not ended for it. It is never backed for a decision: a grant key signs no record, grant or revocation, and no act of it places a member's signature or adopts another act for the collective (reading).

A grant survives the collective's rotations. It ends only by a revocation, a departure emptying its area, a fork or a closing (reading).

**A grant from an area** (field 5) is signed by the collective and completed by signature acts of the area's holders (rule 74). Each kind in field 6 must lie within the area's reach: every act it could reach, computed as for overlapping kinds from the agreement in force ("Areas"), is an act the area reaches. Otherwise the grant is invalid.

**A reinstatement** (field 7) is the exception, and **is a clone of the ended grant** (G2, F128). Its fields 0 to 6 must repeat those of the grant it names, which must be a grant within the area of the same id; one whose fields differ is invalid. It backs acts only within the refitted area's reach (rule 71). Its key (field 9) is its own, the grantee accepts it again, and it takes nothing on.

**A grant of this collective by its founding terms** (field 8, F124 S1) is written before the collective exists, so founding terms can name it (field 14, field 29). Its grantor is the collective whose founding terms name it. **It is carried by the founding terms, which every founder signs** (F125, D6): signed by a founder, the core not judging which, it counts through them. The collective's key takes over from then on, for every later grant and revocation. The grantee accepts it by its own signature act (F128). It names no area, and reinstates nothing.

**A payee's grant in a deal** (F129, H4). Where a deal names a split service, each payee grants the service a grant key of its own, written in the deal's terms. The grant names "this agreement" by null (field 2, scope 1), as founding terms name "this collective" (S1, D6). So it is written before the deal, and the deal names it: **field 14 lists one grant per payee**.

The grant is a person's grant (rule 73), signed by the payee with its own key, and public. It counts only through the deal: once a deal its grantor signed lists it in field 14 and exists, every party having signed it ("signing the deal signs them", Nobody, allegedly). Before that, it backs nothing. The split service signs to accept each grant, as every grantee does (F128).

Every grant field 14 lists names the same grantee, the one split service, and is signed by a distinct party. A deal breaking this carries none of them (F129 reading 3, F130).

**A backup split service holds grants the same way** (F130, H6; F250). Field 29 lists them, one per payee, grouped by service. Each is the payee's own grant naming "this agreement" by null, signed by the payee and accepted by that service, counting once the deal exists, as field 14's do. Every group is signed by the payees that sign field 14's grants, one each. Each group names a service of its own, never field 14's. A deal breaking this carries none of its grants, field 14's included; a verifier not holding every grant it lists cannot tell, so fails closed (reading, F130). *Which service receives a payment is the split cMIP's, attempt by attempt (rule 17); a backup's grant keys count from the deal's existence, as in a collective, and both services' pointers pass the pointer check (rule 16).*

**A payee's grant counts once a deal its grantor signed lists it and exists, every party having signed** (F130 reading 1). "This agreement" is the deal with the versions it clones, and a grant listed by two deals its grantor signed serves each (F130 reading 2). *"Feels the cleanest" (Nobody, allegedly): the mechanic is the collective's founding grant, and the service is no party to the deal, so leaving it stays a revocation, never a renegotiation.*

**Each payee can revoke its own grant** (type 10, an everyday act, public, rule 73); the other payees' grants stand.

**What a split service's grant key may sign** (F129, H5). One rule holds for every split service, whoever granted it, a person or a collective (F130, H7). A split service's grant is a grant field 14 names, or one field 29 names for a backup (F250), in any version of the agreement (reading): a payee's grant in a deal, or a collective's grant.

**Its key signs only receipts for money coming in under the grantor's own claims and offers.** That is, a Money receipt (type 2) whose payee (field 3) is the grantor, and which is a purchase naming the grantor's own claim (field 9: in a deal, the deal or a version of it; in a collective, its agreement or a version of it), or a payment following it (field 5).

It never signs a receipt whose payer (field 2) is the service, nor a payout the grantor is owed (a receipt carrying a batch, field 8, or naming a split in field 5 or in `objects`), and no other act, save one exception.

The exception is a layer's incoming share with the rail's proof behind it (F235). Where the rail pays an account the grantor holds (a bank transfer to the owner), its grant to the next layer's split service lets that service sign for the share on the grantor's behalf: a receipt whose payee is the grantor, naming another identity's split that pays the service as the receiver of a share held by an agreement of the grantor's naming it (rule 15). **The rail's proof is required on that receipt** (the rail's answer, valid). Never a payout of the service's own split, nor such a receipt without the rail's proof: there H5 and H7 stand whole. *"Yes, the owner can grant whatever the owner wishes to whoever the owner chose. No?" (Nobody, allegedly). Why: H5 and H7 stop a service from forging the payee's own receipt for its own split's payouts; with the rail's proof behind the signature, the forgery is impossible, so the limit stays only where no rail proof stands behind it (the project lead's answer, recorded with F235).* *"One rule for all" (Nobody, allegedly).*

**A grantor may scope its grant more narrowly still**: the kinds it names (field 6), and for a collective the area's reach, still apply before this limit.

*Readings, to confirm (F130): "the service" is every service the grantor's agreement names for the split service, its backups included (F250), since money from any of them to the grantor is a payout; a grant to the same identity that neither field 14 nor field 29 names is no split service's grant, and reaches what its kinds name, the grantor's choice, in plain view.*

*Example (H7): a label owns a quarter of a film; the film's service owes the label 40. The label's own split service, holding the label's grant key, cannot sign "the label received 40": that is a payout the label is owed, under another agreement's claim. The label signs its own receipt once the 40 arrives.* *"If it's a grant, it should use the grant key. Worth noting that whoever uses it still signs from under its own identity but via grant key" (Nobody, allegedly).*

So a payout without the payee's own receipt stays the service's open obligation (rule 30): the service cannot sign "Ben received his payout" in Ben's name. The grant names the grantee's identity, which signed to accept it, so every act of the key is traceable to who used it.

*Reading 4 (F129, confirmed by F130): a payout is a receipt carrying a batch or naming a split; "payments on its offer" are receipts following the deal itself, or purchases of its work, which name its claim anyway; a receipt following a standing offer (type 6), whose format is open, is refused rather than guessed; a receipt received by anyone but the grantor is never backed.*

**Revocation (type 10, F128).** A revocation is a decision of the grantor, signed with its own key and citing the collective's chain as an action does (reading). It names the grant whose key it removes, never a place on the grantee's strand (C6). For a grant within an area, it is completed by that area's holders' signature acts, judged by that area alone (rule 74, Q29).

It is a decision ending powers: **an act signed with the grant key that the revocation's history holds was done within the grant's powers, and binds; any other, racing it or after it, is void** (the tie rule, G1). *"Yes, it's a grant key, scoped" (Nobody, allegedly).*

### Request to a judge (type 25)

*In short: a party asks the judge its terms name to decide a question about an act.*

```cddl
judge-request-payload = {
  0 => hash,            ; the version of the agreement whose terms name the judge
  1 => judge,           ; the judge asked, in the terms' `judge` form
  2 => hash             ; the question: the act it is about
}
```

Its `objects` name the version as `[agreement, agreement]`. It counts only public or sealed to the judge. It reaches each link of the chain of judgment it is public or sealed to, in order (rule 35; review 2.6). Standing is read, as built, as a party who signed the version (mechanic, the build's).

### Liveness (type 12)

*In short: a party's sign of life on an agreement.*

`liveness-payload = { 0 => hash }`: the agreement, named as `[agreement, agreement]`. It counts only public or addressed to the agreement's other parties (OF23 a). In a collective, it counts only where the collective placed it (FR10).

### Release (type 5)

*In short: a release ends the owners' claim on a work and publishes its content keys, so the work becomes public domain (rule 55).*

```cddl
release-payload = {
  0 => hash,                ; the work released, by its hash
  1 => [+ [ agreement: hash, index: uint ]],  ; the stakes in it whose claim ends: the work's owners, past and present
  ? 2 => [+ hash],          ; the work claims binding it to its creators: its creators' history
  ? 3 => [+ [ publication: hash, key: bstr .size 32 ]],  ; the content key of each publication carrying it, published
  ? 4 => [ point: any, keeper: hash ]   ; a timed release (N11): the point on the time reference, and who delivers the keys then
}                           ; field 3 is present unless field 4 is
```

It is signed by one direct owner of the work, with its own key. Every other holder whose signature the release rule asks for adds a signature act naming it (type 1). Its inside names, in `objects`, each agreement whose stake it ends, as chain and predecessor.

**A release is public** (its content key on its outside), so that the content keys it carries are published.

A release is complete when:

- it is valid under Identity, and public;
- each stake it names is a stake of that agreement whose object is the work;
- the release rule of each such agreement in force (field 24; absent, every holder) is met among that stake's holders.

**Every direct owner of the work signs, departed holders of a stake in the work included, unless the release rule says otherwise** (F124, N7).

A holder that is a collective signs by its own rules. Its signature (the release itself, or its signature act on it) counts only with its consent. A release, or a collective's signature on one, is reached by the lanes of every layer a release touches, Envelopes, Money and Agreements, each lane that exists meeting its threshold (rule 60, Q7). *Two levels of ownership: the collective owns the work; its members and departed holders own shares of its income, not the work. Releasing is a business decision of the collective, like setting a price to zero, which departed holders, passive, accept; it affects every holder alike.*

**A timed release** (F124, N11) names a point on the time reference of each agreement whose stake it ends, which must name one, and the identity that delivers the content keys then (field 4). The claim ends at that point, as the time reference judges it, and is undetermined where it answers undetermined. *Stated cost (N11): the keys' delivery rests on that identity; if it vanishes, the work is released in Agreements but its key may never come out; the owners may always publish the key earlier. That identity is named for the keys alone, not as a keeper in rule 37's sense, which never receives content keys as such (rule 43; F125 reading 8).*

*N12: every stake in a work lives in its one claiming agreement and its transfers on that agreement's chain, so a release signed by every holder there is complete; a claim the release does not name, by another identity, stays openly contested beside it (rule 7), and the release cannot end it.*

### Negotiation message (type 18)

*In short: a negotiation message is one message in the talk between two sides that leads to a deal.*

```cddl
negotiation-payload = {
  0 => tstr,                ; the message, as canonical text (Text MIR)
  ? 1 => hash               ; format: a text format cMIP, as a text act may name one (Text MIR)
}
```

Signed by one side of the negotiation, with its own key (F118).

A thread is identified by its first message:

- the first message names no chain in `objects`;
- every later message names, in `objects`, the thread and the message it follows, `[[thread, previous]]`, the previous message being the thread's first message or another negotiation message of the same thread.

The two sides of a thread are the signer of its first message and the first other identity to sign a message of it. **A message signed by anyone else is no part of that thread's record.**

Each message's `acks` name the latest message of the other side that its signer has received, and nothing else. A side that has received nothing from the other side yet carries no `acks`.

Its text is shown as a text act's is, plain text always available (Text MIR rules 3 to 5, rule 4a).

*A text act carrying acknowledgements is invalid (Envelopes rule 4a, F110), so negotiation lives on Agreements' own layer, as F110's reasoning assumed: a reaction or a text reply can never be a negotiation message (F118).*

*Stated cost (F118):* a client that does not implement Agreements shows a negotiation message as unknown, never as text; a negotiation needs an Agreements client, which it needs anyway to sign the deal it leads to.

### Contest (type 14)

*In short: a contest answers an act: a party declared absent answers the declaration, or someone who signed onto a vow contests its sales. It shows the dispute, and voids nothing.*

```cddl
contest-payload = {
  0 => hash,                ; the act it answers: a declaration of absence (type 13); or, for a vow, the sale it contests (F253)
  ? 1 => tstr / [hash, any] ; for a vow only: the promise not kept, in words (canonical text) or in a cMIP's terms (F253)
}
```

A contest of a declaration is signed by the party the declaration names, with its own key (BQ4). Its inside names the declaration as both chain and predecessor, `[[declaration, declaration]]`, as a signature does.

**It shows that party's presence and the dispute, and voids nothing** (rule 88, F172). It is shown beside the declaration, to everyone who reads it. A voice the declaration removed comes back only by a later version naming the party. A contest signed by anyone else shows nothing.

*Client conformance:* the declared member's client lets them sign it, and publishes it.

A contest of a vow (F237, F240; **new in draft 11, F253**): field 0 names **the sale it contests**: the buyer's payer's claim of the payment that is a purchase under an offer naming the vow, or, for an offer at no price, the signature act by which the buyer signed onto it (rule 13). Its inside names the vow by its name (its genesis id) as chain and predecessor, `[[vow, vow]]`. Field 1, optional, says which of the vow's promises was not kept (reading (Q3): a buyer may know only that something went wrong). Signed by the buyer of that sale, it marks that sale contested, and voids nothing. Signed by anyone else, it shows nothing. *Mechanics, this draft's, to confirm:* the field numbers and the vow in `objects`. *Built (`docs/agreements-draft-11-build.md`):* the code reads this form; draft 10's, field 0 the vow's name, now names no sale.

*The format is written for the two cases decided; a contest of another act, or by another with standing (rule 54), keeps its format open: "as new needs are discovered the grammar needs definition" (Nobody, allegedly).*

### Settlement request (type 22) and fork settlement (type 23)

*In short: when a deal forks, a party who signed it asks the deal's judge of forks to settle it, and the judge's settlement keeps one branch and drops the rest.*

```cddl
request-payload = {
  0 => hash                 ; the reference: the version of the deal with two complete versions of it
}
settlement-payload = {
  0 => hash,                ; the settlement request (type 22) that activated the judge
  1 => hash,                ; the version of the fork it keeps: its line, and what grows on it, in force from then on
  2 => [+ hash]             ; every tip it drops, ascending, none twice, none the version kept (QF3, F190)
}
```

A settlement request is signed by a party who signed the reference, with its own key. Its inside names the reference as chain and predecessor, `[[reference, reference]]`.

A fork settlement is signed, with its own key, by the judge of forks the reference names (field 27, one of field 13: QF2, F190), or by the link of its chain of judgment that took over (field 21; QG4). Its inside names the request as chain and predecessor, `[[request, request]]`.

How they settle a deal's fork is rule 47 (DQ8, F192).

*A settlement that names no request, or a request by someone who did not sign the reference, settles nothing: the judge never acts on its own initiative.*

*Field 1 is any version of the fork, usually the tip of the branch the judge keeps as it saw it; a version made on that branch after it is on the line kept (wording settled by the project lead, under Nobody, allegedly's delegation of technical wording: what is accepted does not change).*

### Resignation (type 16)

*In short: a resignation is how a member leaves a collective, giving up their voice in its agreement. A stepping down gives up only one area.*

```cddl
resignation-payload = {
```
