- A rotation declaring nothing new: the payment cMIP the Money lane's holder adopted by a record stays in force, receipts on its rail Modules counting with that holder's signature under the new key (Flaw B1): 3.
- The Development lane's holder adopts an extension alone; one acting on Money needs the Money lane's holder too; an under-declared extension binds nothing beyond the lanes that approved it (F106): 3.
- An extension declaring Money in its specification (Development, field 10) is dropped only with the Money lane's holder too (Q18): 3.
- A specification named for tasks of two layers held by different areas: its acts need both lanes' holders, and naming it for the second layer needs both lanes' power (R4): 3.
- The Agreements lane's holder adopts a grant-limits cMIP alone, and cannot replace the time reference or anchoring cMIP (Q15): 3.
- A holder leaves an area by nomination, by proposal or by redraw; a holder steps down at once and the area is frozen until the change (Q13): 3.
- A holder of an area held by two steps down: the other carries on, and the area's grants with it; the only holder steps down: the area and its grants stop until the refit (Q17): 3.
- A constitutional clone removes one area and reorders the others: a grant keeps falling in the area of its id; a clone giving a new area a retired id is invalid (Q32): 3.
- A member removed keeps a quarter of the collective's income as a departed holder: no voice, the current judges applying; a clone lowering that stake without the holder's signature stays a draft; every payout matching its stake exactly, a deviation either way shown; every split delivered to every holder it pays, naming each fee and who received it (F121, Q8, Q9, rule 68, F124 N5, N10): 3.

#### Members joining and leaving

- A collective with a threshold key grammar signs a receipt; a member leaves and the others rotate; the member who left, with another, signs an act an area reaches, and it does not count (F100): 3.
- A member resigns alone and keeps their stake; a member removed under a constitution that allows it keeps their stake (Q6): 3.
- Under a constitutional rule of two of three, a removal completes without the removed member, the seat part of their plan going with it and its stake part staying; dropping the stake part too needs every member (F124 M1): 3.
- A constitutional change rule of three of three, one voice removed: the two remaining meet it (flaw C): 3.
- Three of three: a member signs a clone and resigns; the clone still needs both others (Q23): 3.
- A resignation takes effect at the record registering it, not before; a record registering a departure and writing a clone judges the clone with the departure in effect (A1, C3): 3.
- A record writing an incomplete clone and registering a resignation: the clone is not in force, the resignation takes effect at the record (Q36): 3.
- An old resignation never registered, then a constitutional clone naming its signer that they signed: registered later, it takes nothing (F195): 3.

#### Grants

- A grant hands its grantee a grant key, which the grantee accepts by signing; the grantee's acts, signed with it, are the collective's own, citing the grant; one not citing it, or beyond its reach, is not backed; a revocation, completed by the area's holder, removes the key: an act its history holds binds, one racing it or after it is void, one the collective adopts binds (F128, G1, A6): 3.
- A person grants an agent a key and revokes it by everyday acts, public, with no rotation; an act the revocation's history holds binds, one racing it is void; a grant a thief made with the stolen signing key dies at the owner's rotation; a private grant backs nothing (F128, extended): 3.
- The label revokes Marco; deal 4, void, is adopted by the label's own acknowledgement, and Sofia manages it under her own grant and key; no handover (F129, H3): 3.
- A grantee's act that one device of the collective cites, and a revocation from another device that never heard of it: the act binds, adopted; the grantee's later acts are void (F131, IT2a): 3.
- A departure empties an area: a grantee's act the line's history holds binds; acts racing it, or after it, are void; one the collective acknowledged binds; a reinstatement after the refit, a clone of the ended grant with its own key, backs new acts and takes nothing on (F128, G2, A6): 3.
- The collective acknowledges a void act of a revoked grant's key after the revocation: it binds, adopted (A6, C6, F128): 3.
- A frozen area's grants end; after the refit, only the grants the new holder reinstates, by cloning, back acts (Q22, G2): 3.
- In an area held by two deciding together, one holder alone does not reinstate a grant; both do (Q27): 3.
- A signature act on an ended grant reinstates nothing; a reinstatement act, completed by the refitted area's holders after the refit, reinstates it; one made before the refit counts for nothing (Flaw N, Q22, Q27): 3.
- A revocation of a grant within the refitted area counts only with that area's holder's signature act, although no area of the collective reaches revocations (Q29): 3.

#### Succession

- A seat passes automatically when every voice signed that plan; a plan cloned by two of three makes the successor a nomination (Q14): 3.
- The automatic succession clone puts the successor in the departed member's place in the parties and in every holding of the key grammar, every threshold unchanged, and drops the executed plan; one that changes a threshold, leaves the successor out of a holding or keeps the plan is invalid; the rotation declaring it re-deals the keys, and the successor's voice then counts (Flaw B14): 3.
- Where the dead member held the chain key alone, the succession clone also carries a plan for the successor, naming their own successor, signed by them in it, and comes into force; one without it is invalid (Flaw B17, rule 59): 3.
- A seat passed by succession leaves the dead member's area to be refitted (Q19): 3.
- A succession clone takes the dead member off their areas; an area left empty is frozen; founding terms with an empty area are invalid (Q21): 3.
- A succession clone leaves an area's number above its one remaining holder, who meets it alone (Q26): 3.

#### Abandonment

- Abandonment declaration signed by the named authority, an identity, under the clause the party signed; contest by a contest act: 1.
- Liveness act: 1.
- A deal whose abandonment clause names a threshold of the other parties is invalid; the parties name one identity, a keeper's operator or a collective they form (Flaw B19): 1.
- A founding agreement leaving a member with constitutional power uncovered by the abandonment clause is rejected; a member with constitutional power dies, is declared absent, loses their voice, and the others change the constitution (F105): 3.
- A clone signed by a member later declared absent, already on the record, stays in force; their unrecorded, unacknowledged signature on another clone no longer counts; a year of receipts they signed before the collective's line keeps their signature (Q28, Flaw L, C7): 3.
- A threshold abandonment authority of two of the other parties, one of them resigned and registered before the declaration's line: the one who remains declares alone (Q37, flaw C): 3.
- A threshold abandonment authority of two of the other parties, both counted: one signs the declaration and the other a signature act naming it, which the collective acknowledges before its line or in the record registering it; the declaration counts there; a signature it acknowledges only after that record completes nothing there, and a later record registers the declaration (B15): 3.
- A threshold abandonment authority of two of the other parties against the only holder of the signing key: the recovery rotation removing them names the second signature act, and the declaration counts there; one that names none, the second signature placed nowhere before it, leaves the declared member counted; one naming another act puts nothing in force (Flaw B18): 3.
- A declaration naming another clause version than the one the absent member signed last, an outcome the clause does not allow, or signed by someone other than the authority registers nothing (B12, rule 87): 3.

#### Forks of records

- Two registrations of one departure drawn concurrently: an act after one of them only counts as made after the departure; two sibling clones recorded concurrently leave their parent in force (A4): 3.
- Two sibling clones recorded on concurrent lines: the concurrency rule being withdrawn (step 12b), the parent stays in force until a clone recorded after both resolves it (Q38, A4): 3.
- A clone of one branch of a fork, recorded after both lines, resolves it; one recorded after only one line does not (B11): 3.

#### How a collective ends

- A group splits off with the powers to do so: an ordinary membership change, its members departed holders (F121, rule 91, shape A): 3.
- A collective forks: each side founds its successor first; the fork act names them; every member signs it with their own identity, by a chain signature with their chain key (F132), on a side, under the constitutional rule; the original's later acts count for nothing; its ownership passes to the successors by the members' stakes; the departed holder keeps their share in each; the fork hands out every debt in the history it cites, an unpublished one included, or does not take effect, each successor signing for its own, and publishing that debt later changes nothing; a successor owing such a debt cannot close until it is paid or released; grants end, the split service's included, every grant key at the fork's line, a grantee's deal the fork's history cites binding and any other void; a debt signed off the fork's history is void; open offers are withdrawn; a payment to the old service naming no claim, or the original's claim never recorded by the original's chain before the fork, is no purchase, owed back to the payer; one naming the fork is unrecorded until a successor's chain records it; a member on no side under every-party, a stranger on a side, a successor dropping a departed holder, or two concurrent forks sharing no signer, close nothing (F121, F124 N1 to N4, N13, N14, F127, rule 91, shape B): 3.
- Under a constitutional rule of two of three, two members fork without the third, who signs no side and is a departed holder of each successor at their percentage (F124 N1): 3.
- A fork hands out its whole history: a debt on another device that the line's history joined must be handed out, one on a device it leaves out is void and owed by nobody; while an act the history names is not held, the fork is not complete; a successor's own later fork hands out what it inherited (F127, F125 reading 4 as adjusted): 3.
- The tie rule: a public debt on a device a closing's history does not cite is void, and does not keep the collective from closing; cited, it does (F127): 3.
- A sale recorded on the actions chain: the collective's receipt before the fork, in its history, stays a purchase; one off the fork's history, or after it, is no purchase, refunded (F127, W2, rule 12): 3.
- A fork drawn on a line before a debt the collective had cited: complete, the debt void, the debt and its citation standing as evidence; a member's client that has not pulled every device's latest acts refuses to sign it (F131, IT2b): 3.
- A fork on an old line leaving out a payee pointer, a payment to it and its taking-on inside: the payment still counts (F198); a fork leaving out a binding debt, then a departure registered after it: the fork stays incomplete (F199): 3.
- A complete fork, then a second complete fork naming it: the first stays final, the second counts for nothing (F131, IT1); a second fork naming nothing in its `objects`, signed by the same members: it names the first through their chains, and counts for nothing; a signature act on an ending counts for nothing (F132, U1); two complete closings sharing no signer: neither counts; a third, signed by one signer of each, settles it (U2): 3.
- An old proposal finished late: a member's signature on it, placed after their signature on the ending naming it, counts for nothing; under a threshold, a member who signed neither finishing it is the stated cost (F132, U4); a second fork whose drafter signed the first and leaves it out of its `objects` is no fork, and the first, finished late, ends the collective (U4b): 3.
- A closing sealed to one member takes no effect; public, it does (F128, W5): 3.
- A collective releases its work by its own rules, then, holding nothing and owing nothing, closes; its later acts count for nothing; a closing while it holds the work, or owes anything, does not take effect, and completes once one debt is paid and the other ended by its creditor's release after a partial payment; a release signed by anyone but the creditor ends nothing (F124 N7, N9; F125 D5, rule 92): 3.
- A collective forgives a debt owed to it by its Money lane alone: the release counts once the lane's holder signs, a member holding no Money lane signing changes nothing (F126, E2, rule 92): 3.
- A bankrupt collective settles with its creditor: a partial payment, a share of its work by a clone of its agreement, and the creditor's release for the rest; it then owes nothing, and, still holding its share of the work, stays open (F125, rule 92): 3.

#### A broken collective

- A broken collective (F185): a rotation whose clone's mark is false breaks it, every act of the broken stretch counting for nothing; a resignation in the stretch names the agreement in force before the broken act; a rollback naming the broken act, registering the resignations, under that agreement's constitutional change rule, brings it back, the broken act still shown; a rollback under another power, of another parent, or naming another act, puts nothing in force; no rollback once every voice has resigned; the last voice warned before leaving, and the last constitutional voice (RB5); the broken clone refused as the rollback's clone (F187); grantees' acts of the stretch counting for nothing and the grants working again after the rollback (RB1); payments received in the stretch owed back unless signed anew (RB2); a declaration of absence made in the stretch registered by the rollback with its signatures, an acknowledgement in the stretch placing nothing (RB3, F187); a rotation removing or garbling the declaration broken with the same rollback (RB4); no fork or closing while broken, whatever its line, and money owed back a debt before closing (RB6; F189, 7); a grantee's act after the rollback counting once it cites it (BQ1); what the collective keeps judged by the offer a payment names (BQ2, BQ3); the contest act (BQ4); a returned member's old resignation spent (F189, 1), and an old stepping down once the member holds the area again (QF6); money owed back repaid by a payment naming it, by either side's record, then no longer blocking a closing (QF5); a declaration neither public nor addressed to the member counting for nothing (F189, 8): 3.
- Money owed back from a broken stretch: repaid to the refund rail the payer's own claim names, never elsewhere; to the pointer the payer's own acts hold, never a newer one; to a bare key where its claim says; owed to nobody, it does not block a closing that names it; owed to a payer with no address, a notice sealed to them, its deadline passed, and a closing naming it (QG1, QG2, F197): 3.

#### Made before, made after

- A record naming the latest act of every other sequence of the collective; an act of the collective in a sequence it does not name counts as made after it (F109): 3.
- A signature from a device a member's resignation never mentions: placed by the act of the collective it signs, or the record naming it, it counts when that act is before the collective's line, whatever the device (Flaws E and F, F109): 3.
- A signature placed after the collective's line that only a friend acknowledged counts toward nothing: an acknowledgement by another identity places nothing (Flaw G, F109): 3.
- A record counts only with the signature acts it names; a signature arriving after it completes nothing there, and a later record stays in force (A2): 3.
- A receipt on a branch the collective's line left out counts as made after it, unless the collective's keeper recorded it before the line; a keeper never places a member's signature (C4): 3.
- After the line, the key holders fork from an act before it to backdate a receipt the departed holder of the Money lane signs: it counts as made after the line (F109): 3.
- A departed holder of the Money lane completes, after the line, a receipt the collective signed before it, and it counts (C1); Q23's early signature counts where the collective acknowledged it before its line, and not otherwise (C2): 3.
- The Money lane's holder's own rotation forgets one of their devices: receipts signed from it before the collective's line registering the rotation keep the signature; a signature with the old key on a receipt made after the line does not (C5): 3.
- A clone signed from a device a member's own rotation forgot, recorded before the line registering that rotation: the signature stays valid for the collective, and the clone stays in force (Q33, C5): 3.
- A rotation declaring a constitutional clone with too few of its signature acts named puts nothing in force; a signature arriving after it completes nothing there (Flaw M): 3.

#### Forked deals

- Two complete versions of one deal (F186): the version before the split is the reference while the deal has two branches; an act following either branch counts, judged under that branch's latest version, a purchase included (the buyer is protected); a branch that grows settles nothing; only a complete version naming both branches (field 26) settles the fork, finally, the other branch never coming back; a tangled fork stays on its reference, a buyer under a version every party signed protected (DQ1 to DQ4); a settling version signed by the parties of both branches (DQ5), naming the discarded tip and citing it; only complete versions of the deal read as settling, a draft or a stranger's act changing nothing (F189, 2); the arbitrator settling on a party's request only (DQ8); one numbering across a deal's splits, a gap the holder's alarm (DQ6); a seller's client raises the alarm on a payment naming a version that does not descend from the one it holds, or one it does not hold (F189, 3), and a notice on an older version (DQ7); a buyer's client checks the offer is current; a version beyond the named tip, citing the settlement or not, changing nothing (F192); a tangled deal settled by a version naming every tip it discards, signed by the parties of every branch (QF3); only the judge of forks the reference names settling, none where it names none (QF2); a deal's split with no number, or a repeated one, breaking the plan (QF4): 1.
- A settled deal, then a version on the dropped branch the settlement did not hold, a version grown there by a party left alone, a second settlement by the judge holding the first, and a plain version on either branch: nothing reopens, the line in force stays (F192); a settlement holding a newer version of a branch whose older version it names settles nothing (F188); a settling version naming a version not held is a plain version until held (F196); the judge of forks silent past its period, the next link settles (QG4): 1.

## Open in this draft

Every question and reading draft 10 lists as open ("Open in this draft", with draft 10's rule numbers) stays open as listed there: this rewrite answers none of them. Readings still to confirm that sit inside a rule are marked there.

**New in draft 11.** Writing in F252 to F260 raised six questions (Q1 to Q6 in `docs/agreements-draft-11.md`); all six are answered and written in (F261, F259 revised, the readings of Q2 and Q3, F262, F263, and Q6). Writing the answers raised two more, Q7 and Q8, both answered and written in: F264 (rule 11, a live stream's class follows what is sold) and F265 ("Closing (type 20)", an absentee's share follows the collective's own rules).


**Draft 13** (`docs/f282-f284-build.md`) writes in F282, which decides draft 12's Q2 reading, and F284. Two readings taken writing F284 in are to confirm, marked in rule 14; the second is put as Q1.

**The second pass of core v22** (`docs/core-v22.md`, "Second pass") writes in F270 and F271 and leaves two points open on F270, marked in rule 48: whether "unless the agreement pre-authorised it" reaches the new paragraph (Q7), and which changes of the split plan lower a stake's part (Q8).

## Old numbers to new

Draft 10's rules, in their old order, with their numbers in this draft. Every old rule has exactly one new number; none was split, merged or dropped. The points of rule 64 (was 44c) keep their numbers: "rule 44c.1" is now "rule 64.1". The points of "Made before, made after" keep theirs.

| 2 | 2 | cMIPs |
| 3 | 3 | Commitments of identities |
| 4 | 5 | Positions |
| 4a | 4 | What you sign is what you saw |
| 5 | 6 | Agreement chains |
| 6 | 36 | Named by the agreement |
| 7 | 37 | Records |
| 8 | 38 | Several keepers |
| 9 | 39 | Copies |
| 10 | 40 | Keepers check homes |
